기록상 `mcp-server` capability의 범위 밖입니다.

- `mcp-server`에는 MCP 도구/I/O 계약, Vault parser/writer, 온톨로지 compiler·graph query, 쓰기 안전장치가 포함됩니다. 반대로 **`AST/source search engine`은 명시적으로 제외**됩니다. [mcp-server.md](/Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/ontology/capabilities/mcp-server.md:93)
- 프로젝트는 코드 심볼의 위치·호출 관계를 grep, LSP, AST index, CodeGraph의 역할로 기록하고, Atlas는 의미·capability·검증 경로를 담당한다고 구분합니다. [README.md](/Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/README.md:472)
- 현재 유효한 결정도 기존 `focusPath`가 `symbol coupling`을 주장하지 않으며, 필요해지면 **별도의 `symbol/CodeGraph integration boundary`를 설계한다**고 명시합니다. [DECISIONS.md](/Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/DECISIONS.md:7051)

따라서 기록된 분류는 “`mcp-server` 확장”이 아니라 “별도 심볼/CodeGraph 통합 경계”입니다. 그 별도 경계의 구체적인 capability 정의는 아직 기록되어 있지 않습니다.

    12	display_ko: AI 연결 서버

    15	
    16	# MCP Server
    17	
    18	It provides a stdio JSON-RPC interface so that an AI coding agent can read and safely update local markdown vaults. People and agents use the same file as the source of truth, and the server does not own a separate database or model execution loop.
    19	
    20	## User Outcomes
    21	
    22	- The agent accurately finds meaning nodes in the project and reads relationships, evidence, and impact scope together.
    23	- Upon connection, it verifies actual vault/repository coordinates and tool inventory to detect incorrect folders or stale client states.
    24	- Before writing, it reads the current document and blocks concurrent edits, duplicates, broken relationships, and destructive changes with structured errors and dry-runs.
    25	
    26	## Active Tool Inventory Contract
    27	
    28	- The current set of tools is `TOOLS_FOR_LIST`, which applies annotations and a read-only filter to the registry in `mcp/src/index.js`. Both the `Tool inventory` section of `tools/list` and initialize derive from the same array, so no other document owns a numeric or full name list.
    29	- A read-only server advertises neither write tools nor exposes them in its initial announcement. In both full mode and read-only mode, the header count and the set of read/write names must exactly match those in `tools/list`.
    30	- `mcp-verify` independently compares the live `tools/list` with the counts, classifications, and name sets in the initialize announcement. Documents and configuration screens reference `tools/list` and `mcp-verify`, avoiding promises to users about mutable fixed counts.
    31	
    32	## Identity Boundaries
    33	
    34	- `uid` is the permanent machine identity that persists across renames, while `slug` is the human-readable and editable current address. All node responses return both.
    35	- Exact reads and external interop identities may use UIDs. Markdown relations, URLs, and graph operation inputs maintain slugs.
    36	- rename/reclassify preserves the UID. merge preserves the target UID and records the absorbed UID in `merged_uids`. Standard patches cannot alter `uid` or `merged_uids`.
    37	- `find_evidence` includes `isNode` in all search rows to distinguish graph nodes from standard Markdown. Only rows with `isNode:true` are required to have permanent `uid` and `kind`; standard documents do not fabricate these fields. `nodesOnly:true` is an explicit filter for handoffs requiring only nodes, while default searches do not hide local document evidence.
    38	
     1	---
     2	uid: 0f1364a5-7d0f-4fa5-b913-454d47b33cca
     3	slug: domains/agent-integration
     4	kind: domain
     5	title: AI Agent Integration
     6	display_ko: AI 에이전트 연동
     7	display_en: AI Agent Integration
     8	capabilities: [capabilities/acp-runtime, capabilities/agent-work-visibility, capabilities/cli-developer-entry, capabilities/mcp-server, capabilities/vault-agent]
     9	elements: [elements/agents-destination, elements/vault-agent-panel]
    10	created_by: human
    11	relation_notes: { capabilities/acp-runtime: "Agent integration owns launching the user's already-installed coding agent inside the app over ACP with an isolated config and a vault-scoped permission gate.", capabilities/agent-work-visibility: Agent integration owns the human-visible projection of current and recent AI-agent work across ACP heartbeats and vault activity logs. }
    12	---
    13	
    14	## Definition
    15	Surfaces enabling AI coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor) and developers to read and write the same ontology vault as humans: MCP servers, terminal CLI, in-app connect flow, and the ACP executor layer that directly launches coding agents already installed by the user.
    16	
    17	## Evidence
    18	- README.md: "Your agent reads and maintains it over MCP... one button writes your agent's config and proves the connection."
    19	- mcp/src/index.js · mcp/src/tool-inventory.mjs: coupled boundary deriving `tools/list` and
    20	  full/read-only initialize inventory from active registry
    21	- cli/src/commands/agent-brief.mjs: passing `--project` as explicit project selector matching MCP `agent_brief.project`
    22	
    23	## Inclusion / Exclusion
    24	- Included: MCP read/write tools (`mcp/`) advertising current list at runtime, local CLI,
    25	  in-app connect button, client config writing and `mcp-verify` connection proof,
    26	  ACP executor detection and launching with isolated settings within the app
    27	  (`capabilities/acp-runtime`)
    28	- Excluded: the graph schema itself (that belongs to the graph-modeling domain)
    29	
    30	## Project Meaning Handover
    31	
    32	`finalize_project_meaning` bundles the competency answer for human-readable project Markdown into body digest and graph/source provenance after graph write, vault validation, and project compile.
    33	the sidecar does not store raw answers, raw witnesses, or private source root/remote.
    34	Subsequently, the new MCP process's `agent_brief.meaningAssessment` re-validates against the current Markdown and inventory; if source currentness cannot be confirmed, it closes as `review_required` even if a stored receipt exists.
    35	
     7	
     8	Ontology Atlas is a local-first workbench for understanding a product from
     9	business meaning to implementation evidence. Vault Markdown frontmatter is the
    10	graph, Git is the source of truth, and people judge meaning through plain files
    11	and diffs. Agents use the same vault through MCP/CLI. The macOS app carries the
    12	MCP server; source checkouts run it directly. There is no npm package.
    13	
    14	Atlas complements CodeGraph, grep, AST indexes, language servers, and source
    15	search. Those tools answer structural questions; Atlas provides the durable
    16	meaning layer: task starting point, domain/capability context, evidence, impact
    17	boundary, and verification path.
    18	
    19	The public meta-model belongs only in `docs/ONTOLOGY-ATLAS-SPEC.md` §2/§5.
    20	Do not create competing kind or relation glossaries. The product is
    21	agent-native and human-sovereign: every surface must be consumable as typed
    22	facts by an agent and readable and judgeable by a human.
    23	
    24	The app is the vault's home and the web is a gateway or second-best workbench.

    89	## Constraints: Impact and blast radius
    90	
    91	`impact` and `blast_radius` follow only declared `depends_on`. Containment/domain/element relations are structural evidence for `reachability`/`subgraph`; do not promote to impact or risk. Return `review_required` if no `relation_notes` on declared dependency, `declared_with_rationale` if present. Both are currently not source-backed due to lack of current-source receipt per relation unit; keep completeness and risk as `unknown`. Therefore, 0 dependency declarations are not interpreted as low-risk or no impact.

입력 칸은 최고빈도 표면이고 내 헌장이 그 예산을 `0~--motion-fast` 로 못박는다. `--motion-base`(180ms)는 내가 내 규칙을 어긴 것이다.\"*", "+**반증 조건**: 자람을 켠 뒤 **「타자가 무겁다」는 보고**가 나오면 반대가 옳았다. 그때 답은 임의로 깎는 것이 아니라 `--motion-fast`(120ms)로 **한 단 내리는 것**이고, 그래도 무거우면 전이를 제거한다. **소유자가 호소한 잘린 글자를 실제로 없애는 것은 자람이 아니라 스크롤 배수 정렬**이므로 이 반대가 이겨도 증상은 안 돌아온다.", "+", "+**2. 상호작용석 — 드롭다운을 뒤로 미루면.**", "+> *\"지금 안 고치면 사용자는 `gemma4:12b` 하나로 영원히 갇힌다.\"*", "+**반증 조건**: 채택됐으므로 이 반대는 이겼다. 반대 방향의 반증 — 포털 전환 후 다른 표면에서 팝오버 위치 회귀가 관측되면 프리미티브 전환이 성급했던 것이다.", "+", "+**3. 작업대석 — 웹 CORS 관측의 수명.**", "+> *\"「웹에서 Ollama 가 CORS 를 막지 않는다」는 이 기계의 이 순간의 기본값일 뿐이다. Ollama 가 `OLLAMA_ORIGINS` 기본값을 강화하면(보안 커뮤니티가 이미 요구해 온 방향) 이 관측은 뒤집힌다.\"*", "+**반증 조건**: Ollama 릴리스가 기본 오리진 정책을 좁히면 관측이 뒤집힌다. **그래서 문구를 「CORS 때문에 안 된다」가 아니라 「이 러너의 CORS 설정과 무관하게, 이 앱이 웹에는 전송 경로를 두지 않기로 선택했다」로 썼다** — 기본값이 바뀌어도 참인 문장이다. 웹 게이트를 여는 조건은 **po-steward 판정 대기**(질문 3, 미도착 — 도착 시 후속 기록).", "+", "+**4. 지킴이 — 코드 존재가 동작 증명은 아니다.**", "+> *\"쓰기 파이프라인이 코드상 있다고 해서 사용자 경험상 작동하는 건 아니다. 코드 존재만으로 「신설 아님」을 단정하는 건 이르다.\"*"]}, {"hash": "e80c3cc1f", "msg": "fix: 첫 실행 카드에서 가장 센 잉크가 남의 샘플 크기였다 (#831)", "ago": "5 hours ago", "concepts": [{"slug": "capabilities/first-run-starter", "kind": "capability"}], 막대'", "+   어때?\"*). **이것은 오늘 화면을 의도적으로 바꾸는 유일한 값이다.**", "+   - **사라지는 것**: 접힌 부모마다 상시로 떠 있던 `+N` 알약과 그 점선 목줄.", "+   - **대신 오는 것**: **고른 노드 바로 위**에 도킹된 `+N` 막대. 안 고르면", "+     아무것도 없다. 접힌 개수 자체는 노드 몸통의 각인 숫자가 계속 말한다.", "+   - **왜**: 알약은 셋 중 유일하게 **자리를 «찾아야»** 하는 컨트롤이다. 실측", "+     캡처(2026-08-01, 샘플 볼트 8도메인)에서 「마케팅」의 `+17` 은 자기 노드에서", "+     150px 아래로, 「상품」의 `+16` 은 화면 최상단으로 밀려나 있었다 — 화면이", "+     **누구의 버튼인지**를 더 이상 말하지 않는다. 막대는 탐색을 없앤다: 매번", "+     같은 자리에 있으면 눈이 찾지 않는다.", "+3. **나머지 넷의 기본값은 오늘 그대로다** — 확장 구조 `disc`(나선 원반) ·", "+   한 번에 여는 개수 24 · 이름을 시도할 개수 8 · 동시에 펼쳐 둘 부모 3.", "+   세 숫자는 **새 값이 아니라 이미 코드에 있던 상수**(`EGO_NEIGHBOR_LIMIT` ·", "+   `DISC_LABEL_TOP_K` · `MAX_EXPANDED_PARENTS`)이고, 이제 그 상수들이 설정의", "+   기본값을 **가져다 쓴다**(값이 두 곳에 적히지 않게 — Carbon).", "+4. **셋을 다 설정으로 내보낸다.** 「고르라고 만든 docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B2-on.txt:2618:docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:10112:src/entities/docs-vault/data/content.json-16-  "FOUNDATIONS": "# FOUNDATIONS — what grounds ontology-atlas\n\n> This document ties the product to **public, citable academic/standard/craft references** rather than *feelings*.\n> A collection of grounds to prevent \"building without even knowing what ontology is\" — what already exists, and where/how we make *our own thing* differently on top of it.\n>\n> **Citation discipline:** All references cited here are public papers / W3C standards / public books / public blogs.\n> The initial set was re-verified with original text and official metadata on 2026-06-01, and the ontology-construction reinforcement set on 2026-08-09. Links/citations are provided, but the body text is not copied. When adding new references, maintain public, citable, and directly verified standards, and do not cite materials read only via search results.\n\nThis is a living document. When we make a design or feature decision, we should be able to point\nat *which* of these it descends from — or argue explicitly why we diverge.\n\n---\n\n## 1. What an ontology actually is\n\nThe word is older than software (Aristotle's *categories of being*), but our usage is the\n**information-science / knowledge-representation** one.\n\n- **Gruber (1993)** gave the most-cited definition: *\"an ontology is an explicit specification of a\n  conceptualization\"* — a representational vocabulary (classes, relations, functions, objects) for a\n  shared domain, designed so knowledge can be **shared and reused** across AI systems.\n  → Our vault's markdown frontmatter *is* \"an explicit specification of a conceptualization\" of a\n  codebase. The exact Atlas application model — five authorable kinds, one reserved reader kind,\n  relation support layers, `is_a` test, and inference boundary — lives only in\n  [`ONTOLOGY-ATLAS-SPEC.md` §2](ONTOLOGY-ATLAS-SPEC.md#2-the-five-authorable-node-kinds-and-reserved-reader-kind).\n  The name is earned by that shared meaning model, not by pretending the files implement a standard\n  they do not.\n- **Studer, Benjamins & Fensel (1998)** sharpened it to the canonical four-part definition:\n  *\"a **formal, explicit specification** of a **shared conceptualization**.\"* Word by word:\n  *conceptualization* = an abstract model of phenomena; *explicit* = concept types and constraints\n  are defined, not implied; *formal* requires a representation with defined semantics, not merely text\n  that a parser can read; *shared* = held in common rather than private.\n  → Atlas is explicit, shared, and machine-parseable. Its current Markdown schema does **not** claim\n  model-theoretic semantics, logical completeness, or a DL reasoner, so “machine-readable = formal” is\n  not our claim. This distinction is a trust boundary, not a downgrade.\n- **Noy & McGuinness, \"Ontology Development 101\" (Stanford, 2001)** is the practical methodology:\n  define classes + a hierarchy, define properties (slots) + constraints (facets), then instances —\n  and crucially, *\"ontology development is iterative, there is no single correct ontology, and the\n  right design depends on the intended application and anticipated extensions.\"*\n  → This is our **grow-the-vault-each-session, agent-maintained, no-one-true-model** stance, verbatim.\n\n### The formality spectrum — and where we deliberately sit\n\nThe W3C Semantic Web stack is the standard reference frame:\n\n- **RDF 1.1 (2014 Recommendation)** — RDF graphs are sets of subject-predicate-object triples whose\n  terms are IRIs, blank nodes, or datatyped literals. RDF 1.2 reached Candidate Recommendation Snapshot\n  on 2026-04-07; that work-in-progress status is not an endorsement or an Atlas compatibility claim.\n  An Atlas edge can be *mapped* to a triple-like statement, but the vault itself is not an RDF\n  serialization and does not inherit RDF identity or entailment semantics.\n- **OWL 2 (2012)** — the **heavyweight** end: classes/properties/individuals with **description-logic\n  reasoning** (consistency checking, classification, inference) under an open-world assumption. Atlas\n  does not adopt OWL vocabulary, class/individual semantics, or inference. Missing Atlas evidence is\n  `unknown`/a visible gap according to our application contract, not an OWL entailment result.\n- **SKOS (2009)** — the **lightweight** end: `skos:Concept` + `broader`/`narrower`/`related`.\n  `skos:broader` names a direct broader link and is not itself transitive; Atlas borrows that caution,\n  not SKOS semantics or conformance.\n- **SHACL 1.0 (2017 Recommendation)** — validates an RDF data graph against a separate shapes graph\n  and returns a validation report; it does not make OWL a closed-world schema language. SHACL 1.2 Core\n  was still a Working Draft on 2026-08-03. Atlas's validator plays an analogous product role over\n  Markdown, but it is an Atlas schema validator, not a SHACL processor.\n\n> **Our position on the spectrum:** a lightweight, application-specific ontology and executable\n> meaning model — more structured than tags, deliberately less expressive than RDF/OWL. Human-and-agent\n> readable Markdown, typed application relations, deterministic validation/query, no reasoner, no\n> backend. “Graph-shaped” describes the implementation; it does not assert standards conformance.\n\n### Construction is a requirements-and-tests lifecycle, not noun extraction\n\nThe literature converges on one discipline Atlas adopts: **start from the decisions the ontology must\nsupport, not from a directory tree or a list of nouns.** Grüninger & Fox make competency questions\nrequirements and benchmarks; NeOn and LOT put requirements, reuse, implementation, publication, and\nmaintenance in one lifecycle; SAMOD turns a motivating scenario, CQs, a glossary, exemplar data, and\nqueries into a regression-carrying test case.\n\nAtlas translates that lineage into an eight-stage, local-first construction contract:\n\n1. **Purpose and authority** — name the product outcome, intended users/decisions, scope, non-goals,\n   domain-language owners, and sources before proposing concepts.\n2. **Requirements as competency questions** — derive CQs from motivating scenarios; give each an\n   audience, expected answer shape, required witness kinds, explicit unknown/refusal behavior, and\n   owner approval. An LLM may draft a CQ, but may not approve its own requirement.\n3. **Evidence inventory and reuse** — separate direct, triangulated, structural, conflicting, and\n   absent evidence; search the existing vault before minting a term. A folder name is not a domain and\n   an import edge is not automatically a business dependency.\n4. **Small conceptual slice** — propose the smallest project→domain→capability→element slice that\n   answers a coherent CQ set. Give every concept an intensional definition, includes/excludes, examples,\n   counterexamples, and uncertainty. Do not expand the schema to fill a template.\n5. **Semantic and structural tests** — reject circular definitions, duplicate siblings, category\n   mistakes, dangling references, predicate-direction mistakes, and unsupported `is_a`. Same domain,\n   naming similarity, or folder nesting alone never proves subsumption.\n6. **Functional and pragmatic tests** — replay CQs against exemplar facts and expected answers, then\n   test whether a source-hidden person/agent can make the intended decision with bounded calls and\n   supported claims. A valid file is not necessarily a useful ontology.\n7. **Human acceptance and provenance** — persist only the accepted write plan; keep source spans,\n   graph/source digests, rejected/partial CQs, and the responsible human decision visible. Receipts are\n   provenance, not truth certificates.\n8. **Regression and evolution** — after each accepted change, rerun prior CQs, graph/schema validation,\n   source currentness, impact, and handoff checks. Git records change; the ontology still has to explain\n   why the meaning changed and which requirement authorized it.\n\nRecent LLM evidence makes the human-sovereign boundary stronger, not weaker. LLM-generated ontology\ndrafts can outperform novices in bounded studies, but quality remains variable; one 2025 CQ pipeline\nreported only about a quarter of outputs passing scope/relevance and fewer than half as unproblematic.\nThe 2026 IDEA2 workflow therefore separates LLM elicitation from domain-expert validation and records\nthe full CQ revision provenance. Atlas follows that separation: **agents accelerate elicitation,\nconstruction, and repair; people own meaning and accepted requirements; an independent evaluator owns\nqualification.**\n\n### Quality is a vector; no green total may hide a red dimension\n\nOQuaRE treats an ontology both as a software artifact and as a tool that must be useful. Atlas keeps\nthat separation and does not reduce construction quality to node count, fan-out, compiler health, or a\nsingle confidence score.\n\n| Dimension | Atlas question | Required evidence | Fail-closed result |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Semantic correctness | Are kind, boundary, and predicate meanings defensible? | definitions, includes/excludes, examples/counterexamples, relation rationale | reject or `review_required` |\n| Structural conformance | Does the Markdown graph satisfy its declared schema? | parser/compiler/validator results, resolved references | block invalid writes/finalization |\n| Functional adequacy | Can it answer the approved CQs? | expected answer + quantified witness coverage | `partial` / `visible-gap` |\n| Evidence and provenance | Can each business claim be traced to current sources and an authority? | citations, source spans/digests, approval and receipt lineage | unsupported / stale |\n| Pragmatic usefulness | Can each audience make the intended decision without hidden source access? | source-hidden task result, claim ledger, time/calls | unknown / not-qualified |\n| Maintainability | Can a change be reviewed and prior meaning retested? | git diff, impact path, CQ regression, source-currentness check | maintenance action required |\n| Interoperability honesty | Are exports and standards claims limited to what is implemented? | declared format/profile and round-trip contract | no RDF/OWL/SHACL conformance claim |\n\nStructural metrics and automated judges are diagnostic signals, not acceptance authorities. OOPS! and\nOntoClean can expose modeling pitfalls; LLM graph judges can help triage noisy extraction; none replaces\ndomain requirements, exact witnesses, counterexamples, or independent task-level evaluation.\n\nThe re-executable evaluator packet, categorical verdict, privacy boundary, and representative fixture\nare specified in [Ontology Construction Qualification v1](ONTOLOGY-CONSTRUCTION-QUALIFICATION.md).\n\nFor Atlas, the conservative direct-subsumption test is executable prose, not a reasoner claim: both\nendpoints have the same `domain`/`capability`/`element` kind, every valid narrower example satisfies the\nbroader definition, the distinction is not synonymy or membership/part/dependency/sequence, and no known\naccepted concept fits between them. The normative rule and current `broader` write boundary live in the\nsame [meta-model contract](ONTOLOGY-ATLAS-SPEC.md#22-direct-is_a--broader-test).\n\n---\n\n## 2. Agent memory & LLM × knowledge-graph (the live field, 2023–2026)\n\nOur wedge — *\"the AI agent forgets the codebase every session; this is git-native ontology memory it\nmaintains and queries\"* — sits inside an active, recognized research lineage. We are **not** inventing\nthe category; we are taking a specific, opinionated position in it.\n\n**The \"LLM forgets; give it external memory\" premise:**\n\n- **MemGPT (Packer et al., 2023)** — \"virtual context management\": the model pages information between\n  a fixed context window (fast memory) and external stores (slow memory) via self-issued function\n  calls, simulating unbounded context. → We are the **codebase-specific, structured & human-readable\n  version of MemGPT's \"slow memory\"**: the agent pages the repo's mental model in/out through a runtime-advertised MCP surface\n  instead of opaque archival storage.\n- **\"A Survey on the Memory Mechanism of LLM-based Agents\" (Zhang et al., ACM TOIS, 2024)** — the\n  peer-reviewed taxonomy of memory *sources / forms / operations* and *evaluation*. → Lets us name our\n  choices precisely: **structured (KG) over textual** memory, **write-on-task-completion** operations,\n  **human-readable + git-versioned** storage, **codebase** domain. Safest single category-overview citation.\n\n**Memory as a maintained knowledge graph (the closest analogues):**\n\n- **Zep / Graphiti (Rasmussen et al., 2025)** — agent memory as a **temporal knowledge graph** with a\n  bi-temporal model (event time + ingestion time). The single closest published analogue to us: both\n  treat long-term agent memory as an explicit, maintained graph of typed entities + relations, not a\n  vector blob. → **Where we differ:** we get temporality *for free* from **git history + markdown\n  diffs** rather than a dual-timeline DB; our domain is the **codebase**, not conversation; our store\n  is **plain files in the repo**, not a graph DB.\n- **Mem0 (Chhikara et al., ECAI 2025)** — production memory with a plain-extraction mode *and* a\n  graph mode (`Mem0g`); emphasizes token-cost / latency savings from retrieving a small relevant slice\n  instead of full context. → Mirrors our choice to make **the graph the primary artifact** (markdown\n  frontmatter *is* the graph), and supplies our pragmatic argument: a maintained ontology lets the\n  agent fetch a small relevant subgraph instead of re-reading the whole repo each session.\n\n**LLM × KG more broadly:**\n\n- **Pan et al., \"Unifying LLMs and Knowledge Graphs: A Roadmap\" (IEEE TKDE, 2024)** — the canonical\n  framing of three patterns: KG-enhanced LLMs, **LLM-augmented KGs** (the LLM does construction /\n  completion / QA), and **synergized** LLM+KG as equals. KGs are positioned as *explicit, interpretable,\n  editable* stores that compensate for the LLM black box. → This *is* our core thesis. Our vault loop is\n  \"LLM-augmented KG + synergized\": the agent both **queries** the graph for grounding and\n  **maintains/extends** it.\n- **GraphRAG (Edge et al., Microsoft, 2024; + `microsoft/graphrag`)** — an LLM derives an entity graph\n  from documents, pre-summarizes communities, and answers *global* questions flat vector-RAG can't.\n  → The flagship example of an LLM **building and querying a graph as a memory/index layer**; we apply\n  the same pattern to a **codebase**. **Where we differ:** GraphRAG produces a generated artifact store;\n  our graph is a **human-editable, diff-reviewable markdown vault** that is the source of truth.\n- **\"LLMs on Graphs: A Comprehensive Survey\" (Jin et al., TKDE, 2023/2024)** — vocabulary for what we\n  are: a **text-attributed graph** (frontmatter nodes + typed edges) an LLM reasons over (as\n  predictor / encoder / aligner). Grounds the claim that LLMs *can* reason over typed-edge graphs, not\n  only prose.\n- **\"LLM-empowered Knowledge Graph Construction: A Survey\" (Bian, 2025, preprint)** — schema-based vs\n  schema-free KG construction, and explicitly names **\"dynamic memory for agentic systems\"** as a\n  direction. → Our per-kind normalized frontmatter schema is the schema-based pole; \"dynamic memory for\n  agentic systems\" is our exact positioning.\n- **\"Towards Agentic RAG with Deep Reasoning: A Survey\" (2025, preprint)** — \"agentic RAG\": an agent\n  loops over an external store to ground multi-step reasoning. → Frames our loop in current terms:\n  RAG-*enhanced reasoning* over a maintained graph, not one-shot retrieval.\n\n---\n\n## 3. The codebase side — code knowledge graphs\n\nThe other half of our lineage: representing **code** as a queryable graph rather than re-parsing it\neach time. The premise that a codebase's meaning is best captured as a graph of typed nodes + typed\nedges is well-established academic and industrial prior art.\n\n- **Code Property Graphs (Yamaguchi et al., IEEE S&P 2014)** — fuse AST + control-flow + program-\n  dependence into one queryable graph; express patterns as graph traversals (found 18 unknown Linux\n  vulnerabilities). → The foundational academic precedent for \"**a codebase's meaning is a unified\n  graph you traverse, not re-derive.**\" We fuse `project/domain/capability/element` + edges into one\n  vault the same way.\n- **Glean (Meta, open-source, 2024)** — schema-defined **typed facts** about code in a queryable fact\n  DB, interrogated with a Datalog-style language; powers navigation across a monorepo. → The closest\n  *industrial* analogue. **Contrast:** Glean = machine-*generated* facts; we = **human + agent-maintained,\n  git-native, markdown-as-source-of-truth.**\n- **SCIP (Sourcegraph, 2022)** — a portable, **human-readable**, language-agnostic code-index protocol\n  with stable string symbol IDs. → Validates our choice of a **portable, human-readable, on-disk**\n  representation (markdown frontmatter; slug-keyed nodes) over an opaque binary index.\n- **CodeQL (GitHub/Semmle)** — \"**treat code as data**\": extract a relational DB of facts, query it to\n  find patterns/variants. → The mainstream embodiment of \"**query your codebase like a database**\" —\n  precisely what we offer an agent via focused MCP graph queries (`find_path`, `find_backlinks`, …)\n  instead of\n  re-reading files.\n- **tree-sitter (Brunsfeld et al.)** — incremental parsing → concrete syntax trees with an\n  S-expression query system; the substrate beneath most code indexers (and our own CodeGraph index).\n  → The **structural** layer (\"what symbols exist, how they nest\"). We layer the **meaning** layer\n  (domains, capabilities, evidence, impact) *on top* — that separation is the point.\n\n> **The gap we fill:** CPG / Glean / CodeQL / tree-sitter answer *structural* questions\n> (what calls what, what's defined where) — machine-derived, exhaustive, regenerated. We hold the\n> **human-and-agent-meaningful** layer: *why* this exists, *which capability* it serves, *what breaks*\n> if it changes — curated (not exhaustive), maintained by the developer + agent together, in git.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Design lineage — restraint as craft, cited\n\nOur \"Linear-restrained\" design language (`docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`, `.claude/rules/design.md`) is an\napplied reading of public, citable design thinking — not arbitrary taste.\n\n- **Dieter Rams, \"Ten Principles for Good Design\" (Vitsœ)** — *unobtrusive, honest, long-lasting,\n  thorough to the last detail,* and *\"as little design as possible\"* (\"Less, but better\").\n  → The rationale for neutral greys + a single indigo and the bans on glow/neon/gradients/glassmorphism.\n  Our whole forbidden-pattern list is applied Rams.\n- **Edward Tufte, *The Visual Display of Quantitative Information*** — **graphical integrity**\n  (representation proportional to the quantities) and **direct labelling** (a legend means the mark\n  cannot explain itself). → Grounds honest, proportional rendering of relations so agent and developer\n  read the *same true* model.\n  ⚠️ **The data-ink ratio is cited here as an aesthetic, not as our judgment rule** — it has been\n  tested and did not survive as one. Inbar, Tractinsky & Meyer (ECCE 2007) had 87 participants rate a\n  standard bar graph against its Tufte-minimalist twin and found a clear preference for the\n  non-minimalist version; Bateman et al. (CHI 2010) found embellished charts were described no less\n  accurately than plain ones and were recalled *significantly better* after two to three weeks.\n  Neither study touches graphical integrity, which is why that half stands. When a seat needs to\n  reject a mark, the rule it must cite is Mackinlay's expressiveness, below — \"this ink is not data\"\n  is an assertion those two papers can be pointed at, while \"this mark encodes no typed fact\" is not.\n- **Jock D. Mackinlay, \"Automating the Design of Graphical Presentations of Relational Information\"\n  (ACM TOG 5(2), 1986)** — **expressiveness** (a graphical language must encode the facts in the set,\n  *and no additional facts*) and **effectiveness** (given several expressive encodings, prefer the one\n  the human visual system reads best, ranking channels after Cleveland & McGill). → **This is the rule\n  the design bench actually runs**, and it is what the Infovis seat's mark → typed-fact table is: a\n  mark that maps to no fact fails expressiveness, and a mark that asserts a fact the data does not\n  contain (a decorative rail implying a category) fails it in the other direction. Effectiveness is\n  why `.claude/rules/design.md` sends bar identity to position/length/order/label rather than hue, and\n  why hue-only separation is a defect rather than a preference.\n- **Wathan & Schoger, *Refactoring UI*** (also the Tailwind authors) — establish hierarchy by\n  **de-emphasizing**, use a constrained spacing/type scale, **limit the palette deliberately**.\n  → The concrete how-to behind our `@theme` token scale, the per-screen gutter/spacing consistency\n  work, and the \"no second coloring system\" rule.\n- **John Maeda, *The Laws of Simplicity*** — *Reduce* and *Organize* (\"make a system of many appear\n  fewer\"); *\"subtract the obvious, add the meaningful.\"* → Justifies the kind hierarchy + typed\n  relations as the organizing device, and the agent's job of maintaining a **lean, high-signal** memory\n  graph rather than dumping everything.\n- **Karri Saarinen / Linear, \"Why is quality so rare?\"** — quality is a deliberate daily choice and a\n  strategic advantage; it spreads by advocacy, not ads; craft = believing it matters + skill to\n  recognize it + care for the user. → The canonical public statement of the philosophy our design\n  descends from — restraint as a *quality* decision that wins against louder AI-generated-UI clichés.\n- **Rauno Freiberg, \"Craft\" (rauno.me)** — exceptional UI is *invisible details* (microinteractions,\n  states, motion, polish) you feel, not flashy moments. → Our bar for hover/focus states, transitions,\n  and the topology's feel — *without* the forbidden flashy patterns.\n- **Emil Kowalski, \"Great animations\" (emilkowal.ski)** — natural physics-based motion; sub-300ms\n  ease-out; **purposeful** (convey state, not decorate); 60fps via transform/opacity only;\n  interruptible; respect `prefers-reduced-motion`. → Concrete, citable rules behind our motion budget\n  (`transition-colors`/opacity, sub-200ms, minimal transform). Justifies how the live topology animates\n  as the agent grows the vault: **state-conveying, not glow/pulse decoration.**\n- **Vercel Geist** & **Radix Primitives** — public reference points for the developer-tool aesthetic\n  (high-contrast accessible color, mono for code/diagrams) and the **\"unstyled accessible primitive +\n  our own theming\"** split we follow (inspector, forms, palette; the recent a11y label-association work).\n\n---\n\n## 5. Our own thing — what's genuinely ours\n\nEverything above already exists. Our contribution is a **specific combination** none of the prior art\nholds all of at once:\n\n1. **Agent-maintained** — the AI coding agent reads *and writes* the graph directly through the MCP server's runtime-advertised inventory,\n   not a human-only ontology editor (Protégé) and not a machine-only fact generator (Glean/CodeQL).\n2. **Git-native, markdown-IS-the-graph** — the frontmatter is the source of truth, reviewable as a\n   diff, versioned by git, no backend / no DB. (Zep/Graphiti = graph DB; GraphRAG = generated store;\n   mem0 = service. We = files in your repo.)\n3. **Live topology as the surface** — the same graph is the *human comprehension surface*: you watch it\n   grow as the agent edits, and you edit it back. (Mackinlay expressiveness + Linear's craft, applied to a KG.)\n4. **Codebase-scoped meaning layer** — not conversation memory (Zep/mem0) and not structural code facts\n   (tree-sitter/Glean), but the *why/owns/impacts* layer **on top of** structure, curated by developer +\n   agent.\n5. **Evidence-bound meaning receipts** — an agent can finalize five competency\n   answers, but the receipt is provenance, not an oracle. Every fresh project\n   brief rechecks the current graph, typed witnesses, and source receipt, and\n   keeps structural readiness, competency coverage, and source currentness as\n   separate categorical dimensions. This follows the same inspectability\n   principle as the vault itself: no combined confidence percentage can hide a\n   stale source or an unresolved witness.\n\n**The honest framing:** *agent memory = a maintained knowledge graph* (Zep, Pan et al., the memory\nsurvey all agree). We take the most **inspectable, lowest-infrastructure** position in that space — an\napplication-specific, graph-shaped meaning model that lives as Markdown in the repo, maintained by the\nagent and judged by the human. It is informed by ontology engineering and Semantic Web standards\nwithout claiming RDF/OWL/SKOS conformance. That is the thing to make excellent.\n\n---\n\n## References (public sources; initial set 2026-06-01, construction set 2026-08-09)\n\n**Ontology theory & standards**\n- Gruber, T. R. 상호작용석 원문: *\"신뢰도가 확정되기 전에 없는 신호를 있는 척 배선하면 「명시적 거짓」(#828 이 이미 잡은 부류)을 새로 만드는 꼴이다. 지금 할 수 있는 최소 조치는 하드코딩을 없애 넷을 동등하게 만드는 것뿐.\"* 작업대석 자신도 같은 위험을 반대 논점으로 적었다 — 파일 존재는 「지금 쓰는 도구」가 아니라 **「예전에 한 번 쓴 도구」**를 가리킬 수 있다.", "+", "+3. **카드 크롬을 이번에 걷을 것인가.** 위계석 「미루자」 vs 체계석 「동반 필수」 — **충돌이 아니었다.** 재보니 서로 다른 크기를 말하고 있었다: 위계석이 미룬 것은 **카피 통합**(두 표면 × 두 로케일), 체계석이 하자는 것은 **컴포넌트 교체**(`StepCard` → `StepRow`, 소비처 3곳, 카피 불변). 그 선에서 갈랐다 — 합집합이 아니라 분할이다.", "+", "+---", "+", "+### chief 가 틀렸던 것 — 셋", "+", "+첫 브리프에서 「AI 같다」를 지문 여섯으로 번역해 넘겼다. **그중 둘이 틀렸고 자리들이 정정했다.**", "+", "+1. **지문 1 「선택지가 아니라 목록인데 버튼이 크다」 — 기각됨.** 위 → 「0/3개 준비됨」 → 526px 아래 「0/3 준비됨」).", "+3. **「오른쪽 여백 188px」 — 재현 안 됨.** 위계석 실측: 팝오버는 880 폭 중앙 정렬(좌 316 / 우 316)이고 드릴인 여백은 좌 49 / 우 27(22px 어긋남). **더 강한 사실이 대체했다** — 드릴인하면 LNB 180px 가 통째로 사라지고 878px 을 시키는 동등한 넷보다 낫다.\"*", "+**반증 조건**: 5초 노출 3인 테스트 또는 실사용에서 **Claude Code 사용자 비율 ≥70%** 가 관측되면 채움이 자기 값을 한다. **그때의 처방은 「넷 다 평평」으로 되돌리는 것이 아니라 「기본이라고 말하기」다** — 채워진 것에 「대부분 여기서 시작해요」를 붙여 **탈락 셋이 아니라 기본 `heartbeat.agent`」가 **30% 이상 불일치**하면 exists 단독 배선은 기각이고, heartbeat 를 우선 신호·exists 를 보조로 바꾼다.", "+", "+**4. 체계석 — 이번 교체가 26건 반려 때와 같은 것 아닌가.**", "+**반증 조건**: `StepCard` → `StepRow` 교체가 소비처 3곳 + import "+**반증 조건**: 채택됐으므로 이 반대는 이겼다. 반대 방향의 반증 — 포털 전환 후 다른 표면에서 팝오버 위치 회귀가 관측되면 프리미티브 전환이 성급했던 것이다.", "+", "+**3. 작업대석 — 웹 CORS 관측의 수명.**", "+> *\"「웹에서 Ollama 가 CORS 를 막지 않는다」는 이 기계의 이 순간의 기본값일 뿐이다. Ollama 가 `OLLAMA_ORIGINS` 기본값을 강화하면(보안 커뮤니티가 이미 요구해 온 방향) 이 관측은 뒤집힌다.\"*", "+**반증 조건**: Ollama 릴리스가 기본 오리진 정책을 좁히면 관측이 뒤집힌다. **그래서 문구를 「CORS 때문에 안 된다」가 아니라 「이 러너의 CORS 설정과 무관하게, 이 앱이 웹에는 전송 경로를 두지 않기로 선택했다」로 썼다** — 기본값이 바뀌어도 참인 문장이다. 웹 게이트를 여는 조건은 **po-steward 판정 대기**(질문 3, 미도착 — 도착 시 후속 기록).", "+", "+**4. 지킴이 — 코드 존재가 동작 증명은 아니다.**", "+> *\"쓰기 파이프라인이 코드상 있다고 해서 사용자 경험상 작동하는 건 아니다. 코드 존재만으로 「신설  **누구의 버튼인지**를 더 이상 말하지 않는다. 막대는 탐색을 없앤다: 매번", "+     같은 자리에 있으면 눈이 찾지 않는다.", "+3. **나머지 넷의 기본값은 오늘 그대로다** — 확장 구조 `disc`(나선 원반) ·", "+   한 번에 여는 개수 24 · 이름을 시도할 개수 8 · 동시에 펼쳐 둘 부모 3.", "+   세 숫자는 **새 docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-D1-off-r3.txt:886:docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-D1-off-r2.txt:207:docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:1842:./src/entities/docs-vault/data/content.json:3:  "AGENT-MEMORY-POSITIONING": "# Agent Memory Positioning\n\n> Current product framing for launch, README copy, and prioritization.\n> Last updated: 2026-06-21.\n\n## One-line Position\n\n**A repo-native memory layer for Claude Code, Cursor, and Codex.**\n\nontology-atlas keeps a local, git-backed mental model of a codebase that AI\ncoding agents can read, query, and maintain through MCP.\n\n## The User Problem\n\nAI coding agents are useful, but they forget the durable structure of a\ncodebase between sessions:\n\n- which domains and capabilities exist\n- which files implement which capability\n- which dependencies matter before changing a module\n- which architectural decisions were already discovered\n- which documentation is canonical and which is stale\n\nWithout a shared memory layer, every agent session starts by re-discovering the\nsame project shape from source files and chat history.\n\nThe minimum supported path is intentionally plain: connect Atlas MCP or run the\nAtlas CLI from Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, or another coding agent, and the agent\nshould already receive a useful workspace brief, graph health check, handoff\npacket, and reviewable memory-diff workflow. No CodeGraph, Serena, language\nserver, grep wrapper, or external source-index service is required for the\nproduct to be usable.\n\nAtlas should not promise to replace code-reading tools. Built-in source search,\ngrep, language servers, Serena, CodeGraph, AST indexes, and similar tools are\ngood at structural facts: definitions, callers, imports, routes, and local\nimpact paths. Atlas helps the coding agent one layer above that: it preserves\nthe task starting point, product meaning, capability boundary, implementation\nevidence, and validation path that explain why those code facts matter.\n\n## Product Bet\n\nThe product is not valuable because it is \"an ontology editor.\" Developers do\nnot want another system they must manually maintain.\n\nThe product is valuable if it reduces the maintenance cost of AI-agent memory:\n\n1. Open a repo and generate an ontology draft automatically.\n2. Let the agent propose mental-model changes after real code work.\n3. Let the developer review those changes like a git diff.\n4. Make the next agent session visibly better because the memory is already in\n   the repo.\n\nThat loop is the product. The ontology graph is the implementation substrate.\n\nThe coding-agent value is therefore not \"Atlas reads all code for you.\" The\nvalue is \"Atlas tells the agent what to inspect, why it matters, and what must\nbe verified before acting.\" Store meaningful implementation evidence as\n`element` nodes and relations; leave exhaustive symbol graphs to deterministic\ncode-intelligence tools.\n\n## Why This Can Matter\n\nThe strongest positioning is:\n\n> Your AI coding agent forgets your codebase. Give it a local, git-backed memory\n> it can read and maintain.\n\nThis is stronger than:\n\n> Local-first ontology graph workbench with deterministic compiler and MCP tools.\n\nThe second sentence is technically accurate, but it leads with implementation\nlanguage. The first sentence leads with the daily pain of AI-assisted coding.\n\n## Success Conditions\n\nontology-atlas becomes a real product if a new repo can show value in one short\nloop:\n\n```text\ninit -> bootstrap -> agent answers better through MCP -> agent proposes sync\n-> developer reviews diff -> next task benefits\n```\n\nTarget: the first visible value should appear within 10 minutes.\n\n## Failure Conditions\n\nThe product fails if users feel they must become ontology maintainers before\nthey get value.\n\nRisk signals:\n\n- onboarding starts with manual node authoring\n- the hosted website feels like the primary product instead of intro/download\n- agent sync is not visible in git diffs\n- `health`, `workspace_brief`, and `maintenance_plan` explain problems but do\n  not lead to obvious next actions\n- bootstrap produces a graph that looks impressive but does not improve the\n  next agent interaction\n\n## What The Recent Engine Work Enables\n\nThe recent compiler/MCP/CLI work is meaningful because it supports the memory\nloop:\n\n- `compile_ontology` turns markdown frontmatter into a deterministic graph\n  artifact with `graphHash`, issue counts, aliases, pagination, and optional\n  indexes.\n- `query_ontology` lets agents ask graph-database-like questions without\n  pulling the whole vault into context.\n- `health` and `workspace_brief` give first-contact diagnosis before an agent\n  starts editing.\n- `maintenance_plan` turns graph cleanup into an ordered work queue.\n- Structured MCP schemas and error codes let agents recover from bad calls\n  instead of parsing fragile prose.\n- `analyze_repo_structure` and `infer_imports` reduce the cost of creating the\n  first draft from code.\n\nThis is the difference between a markdown graph toy and an agent-usable memory\nworkbench.\n\n## Agent Builder Concerns Lens\n\nUse this lens when deciding whether a new Ontology Atlas feature is worth adding.\nThe point is not to copy any agent framework UI. The point is to answer the\noperational problems that experienced agent builders keep designing around.\n\nCurrent research scan, 2026-06-05:\n\n- Anthropic's agent guidance stresses that simple workflows should stay simple,\n  while true agents should be reserved for open-ended loops that need tools and\n  environmental feedback:\n  <https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/building-effective-agents>\n- Claude Code guidance repeatedly treats context as the scarce resource:\n  project memory files, subagents, skills, and task-focused sessions exist to\n  keep the main agent context clean and durable:\n  <https://code.claude.com/docs/en/best-practices>\n  <https://support.claude.com/en/articles/14553240-give-claude-context-claude-md-and-better-prompts>\n  <https://support.claude.com/en/articles/14554000-claude-code-power-user-tips>\n- OpenAI's Codex and Agents SDK guidance puts persistent repo instructions,\n  issue-shaped prompts, MCP connections, approvals, and human-in-the-loop\n  resumption at the center of agent development:\n  <https://cdn.openai.com/pdf/6a2631dc-783e-479b-b1a4-af0cfbd38630/how-openai-uses-codex.pdf>\n  <https://openai.github.io/openai-agents-js/guides/mcp/>\n- Cognition's agent verification writing frames async coding-agent output as\n  incomplete until it includes end-to-end proof artifacts such as labeled\n  screenshots, videos, and pass/fail assertions:\n  <https://cognition.ai/blog/testing-development>\n- Google ADK frames agent infrastructure around structured context, sessions,\n  memory, tools, callbacks, traces, failures, and resumability:\n  <https://adk.dev/>\n- LangChain's memory work separates semantic, episodic, and procedural memory\n  so agents can distinguish durable facts, prior episodes, and reusable skills:\n  <https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/deepagents/memory>\n- MCP frames agent integration as a typed boundary for tools, resources, and\n  prompts rather than an ad hoc pile of API calls:\n  <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/>\n\nTranslate those concerns into Atlas product tests:\n\n1. **Context selection**: Does the feature help an agent get the smallest useful\n   packet instead of flooding the context window?\n2. **Durable memory**: Does it turn an agent discovery into repo-backed markdown\n   that the next Claude Code, Codex, or Cursor session can reuse?\n3. **Tool and relation clarity**: Does it expose which MCP tool, graph query,\n   node slug, relation type, or source file the agent should use next?\n4. **Traceability**: Can a human see what changed, why it changed, and which\n   graph proof or validation command supports it?\n5. **Guardrails**: Does it fail closed before risky writes, stale assumptions,\n   ambiguous relations, or unverified graph rows become committed memory?\n6. **Human-in-the-loop economy**: Does it ask the developer for review at the\n   right point, not on every low-value observation?\n7. **Resumability**: If a session stops, can another agent reconstruct the\n   working state from the vault, git diff, graph health, and saved briefs?\n\nNear-term product implications:\n\n- Prefer \"agent-ready packets\" over long explanations: selected node profile,\n  blast radius, relation preflight, source file evidence, and next command in\n  one copyable bundle.\n- Make MCP connection and graph health visible as runtime state, not hidden in\n  documentation.\n- Treat draft ontology changes as first-class: staged, reviewable, discardable,\n  and backed by validation before they become durable memory.\n- Keep the visual UI quiet by default, but make proof, trace, and handoff\n  available through popovers, collapsible panels, and copy actions.\n- When adding a feature, ask whether it makes the next agent session cheaper,\n  safer, or more accurate. If not, it is probably decorative.\n\n## Positioning Guardrail\n\nUse \"ontology\" in technical documentation and architecture. Use \"repo-native\nmemory layer\" in launch copy and first-contact README text.\n\nThe marketable promise is not that users can draw a better graph. The promise is\nthat their AI coding agent stops starting from zero.\n\nWhen explaining the code side, use this boundary:\n\n```text\nBuilt-in search, grep, language servers, Serena, CodeGraph, and source indexes\nfind code structure.\nOntology Atlas preserves product/system meaning, implementation evidence, and\nthe next verification path for humans and AI agents.\n```\n",

`.agents/mcp_config.json`). 한 사람이 Claude Code **와** Codex 를 둘 다 붙이는 것이 정상 시나리오이고, 배타적 단일선택 위젯은 **그 기능을 숨긴다**. 위계석의 목표(넷이 동등하게 읽힌다)는 **채움 해제만으로 달성**되므로 세그먼트는 목표에 불필요하다. 기능 은폐는 되돌릴 수 없는 손실이라 형태 논거가 이를 이기지 못한다.", "+", "+2. **감지 신호를 지금 배선할 것인가.** 작업대석 「이미 있는 파일 존재 신호로 이번에」 vs 상호작용석 「지금 배선하면 안 된다」.", "+   **적용 규칙: 최소 슬라이스 → 상호작용석 채택.** 상호작용석 원문: *\"신뢰도가 확정되기 전에 없는 신호를 있는 척 배선하면 「명시적 거짓」(#828 이 이미 잡은 부류)을 새로 만드는 꼴이다. 지금 할 수 있는 최소 조치는 하드코딩을 없애 넷을 동등하게 만드는 것뿐.\"* 작업대석 자신도 같은 위험을 반대 논점으로 적었다 — 파일 존재는 「지금 쓰는 도구」가 아니라 **「예전에 한 번 쓴 도구」**를 가리킬 수 있다.", "+", "+3. **카드 크롬을 이번에 걷을 것인가.** 위계석 「미루자」 vs 체계석 「동반 필수」 — **충돌이 아니었다.** 재보니 서로 다른 크기를 말하고 있었다: 위계석이 미룬 것은 **카피 통합**(두 표면 × 두 로케일), 체계석이 하자는 것은 **컴포넌트 교체**(`StepCard` → `StepRow`, 소비처 3곳, 카피 불변). 그 선에서 갈랐다 — 합집합이 아니라 분할이다.", "+", "+---", "+", "+### chief 가 틀렸던 것 — 셋", "+", "+첫 브리프에서 「AI 같다」를 지문 여섯으로 번역해 넘겼다. **그중 둘이 틀렸고 자리들이 정정했다.**", "+", "+1. **지문 1 「선택지가 아니라 목록인데 버튼이 크다」 — 기각됨.** 위 갈림 1 참조. 전폭 블록 넷이 옳다.", "+2. **지문 5 「앰버 배지가 헤더와 다툰다」 — 정정됨.** 위계석 실측: 배지(y=195, 9.5px)는 시트 헤더(y=130, 14px)와 다투지 않는다. 패널 제목(y=196, 11px)과 같은 눈높이이고 그보다 **작다**. 진짜 결함은 「다툼」이 아니라 **같은 사실의 3회 중복**이었다(「누락」 → 「0/3개 준비됨」 → 526px 아래 「0/3 준비됨」).", "+3. **「오른쪽 여백 188px」 — 재현 안 됨.** 위계석 실측: 팝오버는 880 폭 중앙 정렬(좌 316 / 우 316)이고 드릴인 여백은 좌 49 / 우 27(22px 어긋남). **더 강한 사실이 대체했다** — 드릴인하면 LNB 180px 가 통째로 사라지고 878px 을 802px 한 칼럼이 쓴다. 그 자리는 이미 비어 있었다.", "+", "+**교훈**: 소유자의 감각어(\"AI 같다\")를 지문으로 번역하는 것은 chief 의 일이 맞다. 다만 그 번역은 **가설이지 판정이 아니다** — 자리가 실측으로 기각할 수 있게 \"틀리면 정정하라\"를 명시적으로 붙여야 한다. 이번엔 붙였고 둘이 기각됐다. 안 붙였으면 chief 의 오독이 처방이 됐다.", "+", "+---", "+", "+### 기록된 반대 (반증 조건 포함)", "+", "+**1. 위계석 — 채움 해제가 틀릴 수 있다.**", "+> *\"Claude Code 를 채운 건 위계 오류가 아니라 옳은 베팅이다. 사용자 대다수가 Claude Code 라면 70% 를 맞히는 기본값이 모두에게 읽기를 시키는 동등한 넷보다 낫다.\"*", "+**반증 조건**: 5초 노출 3인 테스트 또는 실사용에서 **Claude Code 사용자 비율 ≥70%** 가 관측되면 채움이 자기 값을 한다. **그때의 처방은 「넷 다 평평」으로 되돌리는 것이 아니라 「기본이라고 말하기」다** — 채워진 것에 「대부분 여기서 시작해요」를 붙여 **탈락 셋이 아니라 기본 하나**로 읽히게 한다. 색만 다르면 사용자는 「추천」이 아니라 「탈락」으로 오독한다.", "+**오늘의 정직한 위치**: 사용자 0명 · 근거 0 → **기본 없음**.", "+", "+**2. 위계석 — 번호를 없애면 진도감이 사라진다.**", "+**반증 조건**: 재설계 후 처음 보는 사람이 버튼을 누르고 **「된 건가? 이제 뭐 하지?」**를 물으면 번호는 장식이 아니라 하중을 받고 있었던 것이다. 복구는 번호 원이 아니라 **눌린 버튼의 done 상태가 다음 문장을 말하게** 하는 것.", "+", "+**3. 작업대석 — 파일 존재는 「지금 쓰는 도구」가 아니다.**", "+**반증 조건**: 볼트 10개 이상 표본에서 「가장 최근 exists 파일」과 「가장 최근 `heartbeat.agent`」가 **30% 이상 불일치**하면 exists 단독 배선은 기각이고, heartbeat 를 우선 docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-D1-off-r3.txt:1214:docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-D1-off-r1.txt:696:docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:10112:src/entities/docs-vault/data/content.json-16-  "FOUNDATIONS": "# FOUNDATIONS — what grounds ontology-atlas\n\n> This document ties the product to **public, citable academic/standard/craft references** rather than *feelings*.\n> A collection of grounds to prevent \"building without even knowing what ontology is\" — what already exists, and where/how we make *our own thing* differently on top of it.\n>\n> **Citation discipline:** All references cited here are public papers / W3C standards / public books / public blogs.\n> The initial set was re-verified with original text and official metadata on 2026-06-01, and the ontology-construction reinforcement set on 2026-08-09. Links/citations are provided, but the body text is not copied. When adding new references, maintain public, citable, and directly verified standards, and do not cite materials read only via search results.\n\nThis is a living document. When we make a design or feature decision, we should be able to point\nat *which* of these it descends from — or argue explicitly why we diverge.\n\n---\n\n## 1. What an ontology actually is\n\nThe word is older than software (Aristotle's *categories of being*), but our usage is the\n**information-science / knowledge-representation** one.\n\n- **Gruber (1993)** gave the most-cited definition: *\"an ontology is an explicit specification of a\n  conceptualization\"* — a representational vocabulary (classes, relations, functions, objects) for a\n  shared domain, designed so knowledge can be **shared and reused** across AI systems.\n  → Our vault's markdown frontmatter *is* \"an explicit specification of a conceptualization\" of a\n  codebase. The exact Atlas application model — five authorable kinds, one reserved reader kind,\n  relation support layers, `is_a` test, and inference boundary — lives only in\n  [`ONTOLOGY-ATLAS-SPEC.md` §2](ONTOLOGY-ATLAS-SPEC.md#2-the-five-authorable-node-kinds-and-reserved-reader-kind).\n  The name is earned by that shared meaning model, not by pretending the files implement a standard\n  they do not.\n- **Studer, Benjamins & Fensel (1998)** sharpened it to the canonical four-part definition:\n  *\"a **formal, explicit specification** of a **shared conceptualization**.\"* Word by word:\n  *conceptualization* = an abstract model of phenomena; *explicit* = concept types and constraints\n  are defined, not implied; *formal* requires a representation with defined semantics, not merely text\n  that a parser can read; *shared* = held in common rather than private.\n  → Atlas is explicit, shared, and machine-parseable. Its current Markdown schema does **not** claim\n  model-theoretic semantics, logical completeness, or a DL reasoner, so “machine-readable = formal” is\n  not our claim. This distinction is a trust boundary, not a downgrade.\n- **Noy & McGuinness, \"Ontology Development 101\" (Stanford, 2001)** is the practical methodology:\n  define classes + a hierarchy, define properties (slots) + constraints (facets), then instances —\n  and crucially, *\"ontology development is iterative, there is no single correct ontology, and the\n  right design depends on the intended application and anticipated extensions.\"*\n  → This is our **grow-the-vault-each-session, agent-maintained, no-one-true-model** stance, verbatim.\n\n### The formality spectrum — and where we deliberately sit\n\nThe W3C Semantic Web stack is the standard reference frame:\n\n- **RDF 1.1 (2014 Recommendation)** — RDF graphs are sets of subject-predicate-object triples whose\n  terms are IRIs, blank nodes, or datatyped literals. RDF 1.2 reached Candidate Recommendation Snapshot\n  on 2026-04-07; that work-in-progress status is not an endorsement or an Atlas compatibility claim.\n  An Atlas edge can be *mapped* to a triple-like statement, but the vault itself is not an RDF\n  serialization and does not inherit RDF identity or entailment semantics.\n- **OWL 2 (2012)** — the **heavyweight** end: classes/properties/individuals with **description-logic\n  reasoning** (consistency checking, classification, inference) under an open-world assumption. Atlas\n  does not adopt OWL vocabulary, class/individual semantics, or inference. Missing Atlas evidence is\n  `unknown`/a visible gap according to our application contract, not an OWL entailment result.\n- **SKOS (2009)** — the **lightweight** end: `skos:Concept` + `broader`/`narrower`/`related`.\n  `skos:broader` names a direct broader link and is not itself transitive; Atlas borrows that caution,\n  not SKOS semantics or conformance.\n- **SHACL 1.0 (2017 Recommendation)** — validates an RDF data graph against a separate shapes graph\n  and returns a validation report; it does not make OWL a closed-world schema language. SHACL 1.2 Core\n  was still a Working Draft on 2026-08-03. Atlas's validator plays an analogous product role over\n  Markdown, but it is an Atlas schema validator, not a SHACL processor.\n\n> **Our position on the spectrum:** a lightweight, application-specific ontology and executable\n> meaning model — more structured than tags, deliberately less expressive than RDF/OWL. Human-and-agent\n> readable Markdown, typed application relations, deterministic validation/query, no reasoner, no\n> backend. “Graph-shaped” describes the implementation; it does not assert standards conformance.\n\n### Construction is a requirements-and-tests lifecycle, not noun extraction\n\nThe literature converges on one discipline Atlas adopts: **start from the decisions the ontology must\nsupport, not from a directory tree or a list of nouns.** Grüninger & Fox make competency questions\nrequirements and benchmarks; NeOn and LOT put requirements, reuse, implementation, publication, and\nmaintenance in one lifecycle; SAMOD turns a motivating scenario, CQs, a glossary, exemplar data, and\nqueries into a regression-carrying test case.\n\nAtlas translates that lineage into an eight-stage, local-first construction contract:\n\n1. **Purpose and authority** — name the product outcome, intended users/decisions, scope, non-goals,\n   domain-language owners, and sources before proposing concepts.\n2. **Requirements as competency questions** — derive CQs from motivating scenarios; give each an\n   audience, expected answer shape, required witness kinds, explicit unknown/refusal behavior, and\n   owner approval. An LLM may draft a CQ, but may not approve its own requirement.\n3. **Evidence inventory and reuse** — separate direct, triangulated, structural, conflicting, and\n   absent evidence; search the existing vault before minting a term. A folder name is not a domain and\n   an import edge is not automatically a business dependency.\n4. **Small conceptual slice** — propose the smallest project→domain→capability→element slice that\n   answers a coherent CQ set. Give every concept an intensional definition, includes/excludes, examples,\n   counterexamples, and uncertainty. Do not expand the schema to fill a template.\n5. **Semantic and structural tests** — reject circular definitions, duplicate siblings, category\n   mistakes, dangling references, predicate-direction mistakes, and unsupported `is_a`. Same domain,\n   naming similarity, or folder nesting alone never proves subsumption.\n6. **Functional and pragmatic tests** — replay CQs against exemplar facts and expected answers, then\n   test whether a source-hidden person/agent can make the intended decision with bounded calls and\n   supported claims. A valid file is not necessarily a useful ontology.\n7. **Human acceptance and provenance** — persist only the accepted write plan; keep source spans,\n   graph/source digests, rejected/partial CQs, and the responsible human decision visible. Receipts are\n   provenance, not truth certificates.\n8. **Regression and evolution** — after each accepted change, rerun prior CQs, graph/schema validation,\n   source currentness, impact, and handoff checks. Git records change; the ontology still has to explain\n   why the meaning changed and which requirement authorized it.\n\nRecent LLM evidence makes the human-sovereign boundary stronger, not weaker. LLM-generated ontology\ndrafts can outperform novices in bounded studies, but quality remains variable; one 2025 CQ pipeline\nreported only about a quarter of outputs passing scope/relevance and fewer than half as unproblematic.\nThe 2026 IDEA2 workflow therefore separates LLM elicitation from domain-expert validation and records\nthe full CQ revision provenance. Atlas follows that separation: **agents accelerate elicitation,\nconstruction, and repair; people own meaning and accepted requirements; an independent evaluator owns\nqualification.**\n\n### Quality is a vector; no green total may hide a red dimension\n\nOQuaRE treats an ontology both as a software artifact and as a tool that must be useful. Atlas keeps\nthat separation and does not reduce construction quality to node count, fan-out, compiler health, or a\nsingle confidence score.\n\n| Dimension | Atlas question | Required evidence | Fail-closed result |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Semantic correctness | Are kind, boundary, and predicate meanings defensible? | definitions, includes/excludes, examples/counterexamples, relation rationale | reject or `review_required` |\n| Structural conformance | Does the Markdown graph satisfy its declared schema? | parser/compiler/validator results, resolved references | block invalid writes/finalization |\n| Functional adequacy | Can it answer the approved CQs? | expected answer + quantified witness coverage | `partial` / `visible-gap` |\n| Evidence and provenance | Can each business claim be traced to current sources and an authority? | citations, source spans/digests, approval and receipt lineage | unsupported / stale |\n| Pragmatic usefulness | Can each audience make the intended decision without hidden source access? | source-hidden task result, claim ledger, time/calls | unknown / not-qualified |\n| Maintainability | Can a change be reviewed and prior meaning retested? | git diff, impact path, CQ regression, source-currentness check | maintenance action required |\n| Interoperability honesty | Are exports and standards claims limited to what is implemented? | declared format/profile and round-trip contract | no RDF/OWL/SHACL conformance claim |\n\nStructural metrics and automated judges are diagnostic signals, not acceptance authorities. OOPS! and\nOntoClean can expose modeling pitfalls; LLM graph judges can help triage noisy extraction; none replaces\ndomain requirements, exact witnesses, counterexamples, or independent task-level evaluation.\n\nThe re-executable evaluator packet, categorical verdict, privacy boundary, and representative fixture\nare specified in [Ontology Construction Qualification v1](ONTOLOGY-CONSTRUCTION-QUALIFICATION.md).\n\nFor Atlas, the conservative direct-subsumption test is executable prose, not a reasoner claim: both\nendpoints have the same `domain`/`capability`/`element` kind, every valid narrower example satisfies the\nbroader definition, the distinction is not synonymy or membership/part/dependency/sequence, and no known\naccepted concept fits between them. The normative rule and current `broader` write boundary live in the\nsame [meta-model contract](ONTOLOGY-ATLAS-SPEC.md#22-direct-is_a--broader-test).\n\n---\n\n## 2. Agent memory & LLM × knowledge-graph (the live field, 2023–2026)\n\nOur wedge — *\"the AI agent forgets the codebase every session; this is git-native ontology memory it\nmaintains and queries\"* — sits inside an active, recognized research lineage. We are **not** inventing\nthe category; we are taking a specific, opinionated position in it.\n\n**The \"LLM forgets; give it external memory\" premise:**\n\n- **MemGPT (Packer et al., 2023)** — \"virtual context management\": the model pages information between\n  a fixed context window (fast memory) and external stores (slow memory) via self-issued function\n  calls, simulating unbounded context. → We are the **codebase-specific, structured & human-readable\n  version of MemGPT's \"slow memory\"**: the agent pages the repo's mental model in/out through a runtime-advertised MCP surface\n  instead of opaque archival storage.\n- **\"A Survey on the Memory Mechanism of LLM-based Agents\" (Zhang et al., ACM TOIS, 2024)** — the\n  peer-reviewed taxonomy of memory *sources / forms / operations* and *evaluation*. → Lets us name our\n  choices precisely: **structured (KG) over textual** memory, **write-on-task-completion** operations,\n  **human-readable + git-versioned** storage, **codebase** domain. Safest single category-overview citation.\n\n**Memory as a maintained knowledge graph (the closest analogues):**\n\n- **Zep / Graphiti (Rasmussen et al., 2025)** — agent memory as a **temporal knowledge graph** with a\n  bi-temporal model (event time + ingestion time). The single closest published analogue to us: both\n  treat long-term agent memory as an explicit, maintained graph of typed entities + relations, not a\n  vector blob. → **Where we differ:** we get temporality *for free* from **git history + markdown\n  diffs** rather than a dual-timeline DB; our domain is the **codebase**, not conversation; our store\n  is **plain files in the repo**, not a graph DB.\n- **Mem0 (Chhikara et al., ECAI 2025)** — production memory with a plain-extraction mode *and* a\n  graph mode (`Mem0g`); emphasizes token-cost / latency savings from retrieving a small relevant slice\n  instead of full context. → Mirrors our choice to make **the graph the primary artifact** (markdown\n  frontmatter *is* the graph), and supplies our pragmatic argument: a maintained ontology lets the\n  agent fetch a small relevant subgraph instead of re-reading the whole repo each session.\n\n**LLM × KG more broadly:**\n\n- **Pan et al., \"Unifying LLMs and Knowledge Graphs: A Roadmap\" (IEEE TKDE, 2024)** — the canonical\n  framing of three patterns: KG-enhanced LLMs, **LLM-augmented KGs** (the LLM does construction /\n  completion / QA), and **synergized** LLM+KG as equals. KGs are positioned as *explicit, interpretable,\n  editable* stores that compensate for the LLM black box. → This *is* our core thesis. Our vault loop is\n  \"LLM-augmented KG + synergized\": the agent both **queries** the graph for grounding and\n  **maintains/extends** it.\n- **GraphRAG (Edge et al., Microsoft, 2024; + `microsoft/graphrag`)** — an LLM derives an entity graph\n  from documents, pre-summarizes communities, and answers *global* questions flat vector-RAG can't.\n  → The flagship example of an LLM **building and querying a graph as a memory/index layer**; we apply\n  the same pattern to a **codebase**. **Where we differ:** GraphRAG produces a generated artifact store;\n  our graph is a **human-editable, diff-reviewable markdown vault** that is the source of truth.\n- **\"LLMs on Graphs: A Comprehensive Survey\" (Jin et al., TKDE, 2023/2024)** — vocabulary for what we\n  are: a **text-attributed graph** (frontmatter nodes + typed edges) an LLM reasons over (as\n  predictor / encoder / aligner). Grounds the claim that LLMs *can* reason over typed-edge graphs, not\n  only prose.\n- **\"LLM-empowered Knowledge Graph Construction: A Survey\" (Bian, 2025, preprint)** — schema-based vs\n  schema-free KG construction, and explicitly names **\"dynamic memory for agentic systems\"** as a\n  direction. → Our per-kind normalized frontmatter schema is the schema-based pole; \"dynamic memory for\n  agentic systems\" is our exact positioning.\n- **\"Towards Agentic RAG with Deep Reasoning: A Survey\" (2025, preprint)** — \"agentic RAG\": an agent\n  loops over an external store to ground multi-step reasoning. → Frames our loop in current terms:\n  RAG-*enhanced reasoning* over a maintained graph, not one-shot retrieval.\n\n---\n\n## 3. The codebase side — code knowledge graphs\n\nThe other half of our lineage: representing **code** as a queryable graph rather than re-parsing it\neach time. The premise that a codebase's meaning is best captured as a graph of typed nodes + typed\nedges is well-established academic and industrial prior art.\n\n- **Code Property Graphs (Yamaguchi et al., IEEE S&P 2014)** — fuse AST + control-flow + program-\n  dependence into one queryable graph; express patterns as graph traversals (found 18 unknown Linux\n  vulnerabilities). → The foundational academic precedent for \"**a codebase's meaning is a unified\n  graph you traverse, not re-derive.**\" We fuse `project/domain/capability/element` + edges into one\n  vault the same way.\n- **Glean (Meta, open-source, 2024)** — schema-defined **typed facts** about code in a queryable fact\n  DB, interrogated with a Datalog-style language; powers navigation across a monorepo. → The closest\n  *industrial* analogue. **Contrast:** Glean = machine-*generated* facts; we = **human + agent-maintained,\n  git-native, markdown-as-source-of-truth.**\n- **SCIP (Sourcegraph, 2022)** — a portable, **human-readable**, language-agnostic code-index protocol\n  with stable string symbol IDs. → Validates our choice of a **portable, human-readable, on-disk**\n  representation (markdown frontmatter; slug-keyed nodes) over an opaque binary index.\n- **CodeQL (GitHub/Semmle)** — \"**treat code as data**\": extract a relational DB of facts, query it to\n  find patterns/variants. → The mainstream embodiment of \"**query your codebase like a database**\" —\n  precisely what we offer an agent via focused MCP graph queries (`find_path`, `find_backlinks`, …)\n  instead of\n  re-reading files.\n- **tree-sitter (Brunsfeld et al.)** — incremental parsing → concrete syntax trees with an\n  S-expression query system; the substrate beneath most code indexers (and our own CodeGraph index).\n  → The **structural** layer (\"what symbols exist, how they nest\"). We layer the **meaning** layer\n  (domains, capabilities, evidence, impact) *on top* — that separation is the point.\n\n> **The gap we fill:** CPG / Glean / CodeQL / tree-sitter answer *structural* questions\n> (what calls what, what's defined where) — machine-derived, exhaustive, regenerated. We hold the\n> **human-and-agent-meaningful** layer: *why* this exists, *which capability* it serves, *what breaks*\n> if it changes — curated (not exhaustive), maintained by the developer + agent together, in git.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Design lineage — restraint as craft, cited\n\nOur \"Linear-restrained\" design language (`docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`, `.claude/rules/design.md`) is an\napplied reading of public, citable design thinking — not arbitrary taste.\n\n- **Dieter Rams, \"Ten Principles for Good Design\" (Vitsœ)** — *unobtrusive, honest, long-lasting,\n  thorough to the last detail,* and *\"as little design as possible\"* (\"Less, but better\").\n  → The rationale for neutral greys + a single indigo and the bans on glow/neon/gradients/glassmorphism.\n  Our whole forbidden-pattern list is applied Rams.\n- **Edward Tufte, *The Visual Display of Quantitative Information*** — **graphical integrity**\n  (representation proportional to the quantities) and **direct labelling** (a legend means the mark\n  cannot explain itself). → Grounds honest, proportional rendering of relations so agent and developer\n  read the *same true* model.\n  ⚠️ **The data-ink ratio is cited here as an aesthetic, not as our judgment rule** — it has been\n  tested and did not survive as one. Inbar, Tractinsky & Meyer (ECCE 2007) had 87 participants rate a\n  standard bar graph against its Tufte-minimalist twin and found a clear preference for the\n  non-minimalist version; Bateman et al. (CHI 2010) found embellished charts were described no less\n  accurately than plain ones and were recalled *significantly better* after two to three weeks.\n  Neither study touches graphical integrity, which is why that half stands. When a seat needs to\n  reject a mark, the rule it must cite is Mackinlay's expressiveness, below — \"this ink is not data\"\n  is an assertion those two papers can be pointed at, while \"this mark encodes no typed fact\" is not.\n- **Jock D. Mackinlay, \"Automating the Design of Graphical Presentations of Relational Information\"\n  (ACM TOG 5(2), 1986)** — **expressiveness** (a graphical language must encode the facts in the set,\n  *and no additional facts*) and **effectiveness** (given several expressive encodings, prefer the one\n  the human visual system reads best, ranking channels after Cleveland & McGill). → **This is the rule\n  the design bench actually runs**, and it is what the Infovis seat's mark → typed-fact table is: a\n  mark that maps to no fact fails expressiveness, and a mark that asserts a fact the data does not\n  contain (a decorative rail implying a category) fails it in the other direction. Effectiveness is\n  why `.claude/rules/design.md` sends bar identity to position/length/order/label rather than hue, and\n  why hue-only separation is a defect rather than a preference.\n- **Wathan & Schoger, *Refactoring UI*** (also the Tailwind authors) — establish hierarchy by\n  **de-emphasizing**, use a constrained spacing/type scale, **limit the palette deliberately**.\n  → The concrete how-to behind our `@theme` token scale, the per-screen gutter/spacing consistency\n  work, and the \"no second coloring system\" rule.\n- **John Maeda, *The Laws of Simplicity*** — *Reduce* and *Organize* (\"make a system of many appear\n  fewer\"); *\"subtract the obvious, add the meaningful.\"* → Justifies the kind hierarchy + typed\n  relations as the organizing device, and the agent's job of maintaining a **lean, high-signal** memory\n  graph rather than dumping everything.\n- **Karri Saarinen / Linear, \"Why is quality so rare?\"** — quality is a deliberate daily choice and a\n  strategic advantage; it spreads by advocacy, not ads; craft = believing it matters + skill to\n  recognize it + care for the user. → The canonical public statement of the philosophy our design\n  descends from — restraint as a *quality* decision that wins against louder AI-generated-UI clichés.\n- **Rauno Freiberg, \"Craft\" (rauno.me)** — exceptional UI is *invisible details* (microinteractions,\n  states, motion, polish) you feel, not flashy moments. → Our bar for hover/focus states, transitions,\n  and the topology's feel — *without* the forbidden flashy patterns.\n- **Emil Kowalski, \"Great animations\" (emilkowal.ski)** — natural physics-based motion; sub-300ms\n  ease-out; **purposeful** (convey state, not decorate); 60fps via transform/opacity only;\n  interruptible; respect `prefers-reduced-motion`. → Concrete, citable rules behind our motion budget\n  (`transition-colors`/opacity, sub-200ms, minimal transform). Justifies how the live topology animates\n  as the agent grows the vault: **state-conveying, not glow/pulse decoration.**\n- **Vercel Geist** & **Radix Primitives** — public reference points for the developer-tool aesthetic\n  (high-contrast accessible color, mono for code/diagrams) and the **\"unstyled accessible primitive +\n  our own theming\"** split we follow (inspector, forms, palette; the recent a11y label-association work).\n\n---\n\n## 5. Our own thing — what's genuinely ours\n\nEverything above already exists. Our contribution is a **specific combination** none of the prior art\nholds all of at once:\n\n1. **Agent-maintained** — the AI coding agent reads *and writes* the graph directly through the MCP server's runtime-advertised inventory,\n   not a human-only ontology editor (Protégé) and not a machine-only fact generator (Glean/CodeQL).\n2. **Git-native, markdown-IS-the-graph** — the frontmatter is the source of truth, reviewable as a\n   diff, versioned by git, no backend / no DB. (Zep/Graphiti = graph DB; GraphRAG = generated store;\n   mem0 = service. We = files in your repo.)\n3. **Live topology as the surface** — the same graph is the *human comprehension surface*: you watch it\n   grow as the agent edits, and you edit it back. (Mackinlay expressiveness + Linear's craft, applied to a KG.)\n4. **Codebase-scoped meaning layer** — not conversation memory (Zep/mem0) and not structural code facts\n   (tree-sitter/Glean), but the *why/owns/impacts* layer **on top of** structure, curated by developer +\n   agent.\n5. **Evidence-bound meaning receipts** — an agent can finalize five competency\n   answers, but the receipt is provenance, not an oracle. Every fresh project\n   brief rechecks the current graph, typed witnesses, and source receipt, and\n   keeps structural readiness, competency coverage, and source currentness as\n   separate categorical dimensions. This follows the same inspectability\n   principle as the vault itself: no combined confidence percentage can hide a\n   stale source or an unresolved witness.\n\n**The honest framing:** *agent memory = a maintained knowledge graph* (Zep, Pan et al., the memory\nsurvey all agree). We take the most **inspectable, lowest-infrastructure** position in that space — an\napplication-specific, graph-shaped meaning model that lives as Markdown in the repo, maintained by the\nagent and judged by the human. It is informed by ontology engineering and Semantic Web standards\nwithout claiming RDF/OWL/SKOS conformance. That is the thing to make excellent.\n\n---\n\n## References (public sources; initial set 2026-06-01, construction set 2026-08-09)\n\n**Ontology theory & standards**\n- Gruber, T. R.    468	- **The bundled MCP server is a file, not a service.** It sits inside the app
   469	  bundle and keeps working when the app is closed, because your agent launches
   470	  it itself.
   471	
   472	## What this is not
   473	
   474	- **Not a wiki, and not agent memory.** A wiki only people write rots the week
   475	  it is written; a store only agents write drifts with nobody left to judge it.
   476	  Atlas is one layer both audiences read and write, and the arbiter is a git
   477	  diff. The side-by-side comparison is
   478	  [above](#why-not-just-use-a-notes-tool).
   479	- **Not a code index.** Grep, language servers, AST indexes, and CodeGraph
   480	  answer where a symbol lives and what calls it, and Atlas replaces none of
   481	  them. It answers why that artifact matters, which capability it serves, and
   482	  what to verify before changing it — it tells the agent which structural
   483	  question is worth asking. Curated, not exhaustive.
   484	- **Not an RDF, OWL, SKOS, or SHACL implementation.** Atlas exports a bounded
   485	  graph shape, but its Markdown vault is not an RDF serialization, its validator
   486	  is not a SHACL processor, and its query engine is not a reasoner. A persisted
   487	  relation is a declared claim, never an entailment, and an absent one is a
   488	  visible gap, never a negative fact. The boundary is written down instead of
   489	  implied:
   490	  [specification §5.2](docs/ONTOLOGY-ATLAS-SPEC.md#52-standards-boundary).
    90	the same model
    91	```
    92	
    93	AST/code-index and source-intelligence tools such as language servers, Serena,
    94	CodeGraph, and built-in search are reference points for the
    95	implementation-evidence side of that loop: parse deterministically, index
    96	locally, answer structural questions without re-reading the repo. Atlas should
    97	learn from those properties without copying implementation: local-first
    98	indexing, stable node identifiers, incremental refresh, query-plan style
    99	narrowing, and impact traversal. Atlas' distinct value is the curated business
   100	and product meaning layer on top: what the organization is trying to do, which
   101	capabilities carry it, which implementation proves it, what changes affect it,
   102	and what a human or AI agent should verify before acting.
   103	
   104	The coding-agent promise is not that Atlas replaces source search or stores an
   105	exhaustive symbol graph. Built-in search, grep, language servers, Serena,
   106	CodeGraph, and AST indexes should remain optional structural layers for
   107	definitions, callers, imports, and local code impact. Atlas must work for plain
   108	Claude Code or Codex after only the Atlas MCP server or CLI is connected; no
   109	external code index, source-intelligence service, or extra agent plugin may be a
   110	precondition for first value. Source tools are accelerators after the Atlas
    24	Without a shared memory layer, every agent session starts by re-discovering the
    25	same project shape from source files and chat history.
    26	
    27	The minimum supported path is intentionally plain: connect Atlas MCP or run the
    28	Atlas CLI from Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, or another coding agent, and the agent
    29	should already receive a useful workspace brief, graph health check, handoff
    30	packet, and reviewable memory-diff workflow. No CodeGraph, Serena, language
    31	server, grep wrapper, or external source-index service is required for the
    32	product to be usable.
    33	
    34	Atlas should not promise to replace code-reading tools. Built-in source search,
    35	grep, language servers, Serena, CodeGraph, AST indexes, and similar tools are
    36	good at structural facts: definitions, callers, imports, routes, and local
    37	impact paths. Atlas helps the coding agent one layer above that: it preserves
    38	the task starting point, product meaning, capability boundary, implementation
    39	evidence, and validation path that explain why those code facts matter.

  7040	## 2026-08-13 (2) — 큰 배열의 확인보다 구현 경로 focus가 먼저다
  7041	
  7042	### 반증 관측
  7043	
  7044	같은 날 앞 결정을 반영한 fresh 서버로 무코칭 시험을 다시 했다. Claude Code는
  7045	`reviewMode:"next"`를 골라 13회·overflow 0으로 끝났지만, Codex는 도구 설명에 있는
  7046	두 번째 확인 `{reviewMode:"full",allowLargeResponse:true}`까지 스스로 승인해 3.94 MB
  7047	complete scan을 세 번 호출했다. 최종 답은 좋았지만 transcript가 3.8 MB가 됐다.
  7048	“명시적 확인이면 에이전트가 비용을 이해했다”는 전제가 실제 사용자에게는 성립하지
  7049	않았다. 앞 기록의 해자 자리 반대가 이겼다.
  7050	
  7051	### 결정
  7052	
  7053	- FDE가 analyzer/ontology에서 구현 file path를 하나 얻었으면 complete import graph보다
  7054	  `focusPath`를 먼저 쓴다. `reviewMode:"focus"`를 명시해도 같은 계약이다.
  7055	- focus는 exact file-level static imports를 incoming/outgoing으로 세고, deterministic
  7056	  cursor와 최대 100개 영수증만 반환한다. vault/reconciliation이 없어도 된다.
  7057	- focus는 source boundary일 뿐 runtime blast radius, affected behavior, symbol coupling,
  7058	  test completeness, semantic `depends_on`을 주장하지 않는다. `writeAllowed:false`다.
  7059	- full+confirmation은 호환성과 오프라인 전수 감사용으로 남지만 기본 FDE impact
  7060	  경로로 설명하지 않는다.
  7061	
  7062	### 가장 강한 반대와 반증 조건
  7063	
  7064	가장 강한 반대는 exact path를 모르는 cold start에서는 focus가 아무 도움도 주지
  7065	않는다는 것이다. analyzer의 entrypoint/path evidence가 빈약하면 여전히 global queue나
  7066	source tool이 필요하다. 수정 후 fresh Codex·Claude가 Refined의 feature registration
  7067	질문에서 `source/feature-manager.tsx` focus를 발견하지 못하거나 full을 다시 고르면 이
  7068	결정도 충분하지 않다. 그때는 `index_project` plan에 executable focus call을 직접 싣거나,
  7069	별도 symbol/CodeGraph integration boundary를 설계한다. focus 결과를 의미 관련성 순위로
  7070	정렬하는 일은 하지 않는다.
  7071

  7078	
  7079	### 관찰한 문제
  7080	
  7118	완주하지 못했으며, accountable 집행자가 기회비용을 직접 검토했다. 이 불완전성을
  7119	5석 만장일치로 포장하지 않는다.
  7120	
  7121	가장 강한 반대는 “폭주를 막았을 뿐, FDE가 어디서 시작하고 무엇이 영향받는지는
  7122	아직 못 답한다”이다. Refined의 사전식 첫 후보는 중앙 seam인
  7123	`feature-manager`가 아니라 `action-pr-link`였고, 특정 endpoint의 incoming/outgoing

  7127

  7129	  선택하거나 대량 cursor를 순회하면, 전달 기본값만으로 P0가 닫혔다는 판단은
  7130	  틀리다. endpoint/source-path focus filter를 P0로 승격한다.

  7134	  결합하는 계약을 유지한다.
  7135	- 100+ **후보**를 처리했다는 사실을 100+ **승인된 의미 노드** 품질로 말하지
  7136	  않는다. 100+ node qualification은 별도 사람 승인 plan과 source-hidden 평가가
  7137	  있을 때만 통과한다.

codex
기록상 `mcp-server` capability의 범위 밖입니다.

- `mcp-server`에는 MCP 도구/I/O 계약, Vault parser/writer, 온톨로지 compiler·graph query, 쓰기 안전장치가 포함됩니다. 반대로 **`AST/source search engine`은 명시적으로 제외**됩니다. [mcp-server.md](/Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/ontology/capabilities/mcp-server.md:93)
- 프로젝트는 코드 심볼의 위치·호출 관계를 grep, LSP, AST index, CodeGraph의 역할로 기록하고, Atlas는 의미·capability·검증 경로를 담당한다고 구분합니다. [README.md](/Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/README.md:472)
- 현재 유효한 결정도 기존 `focusPath`가 `symbol coupling`을 주장하지 않으며, 필요해지면 **별도의 `symbol/CodeGraph integration boundary`를 설계한다**고 명시합니다. [DECISIONS.md](/Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/DECISIONS.md:7051)

따라서 기록된 분류는 “`mcp-server` 확장”이 아니라 “별도 심볼/CodeGraph 통합 경계”입니다. 그 별도 경계의 구체적인 capability 정의는 아직 기록되어 있지 않습니다.
